You are given a **0-indexed** integer array `nums`. You are allowed to perform the following operation any number of times: Choose an element `nums[i]` and set `nums[i]` to `nums[i] + 1` or `nums[i] - 1`. The cost of each operation is `1`. Find the **minimum** cost to make all elements in the array equal. Return _an integer representing the minimum cost to make all elements equal._

Note that the function declaration is def min_cost_to_make_equal(nums):. 
GT: def min_cost_to_make_equal(nums):
    """
    Given a list of integers, return the minimum cost to make all elements equal.
    The cost of changing any element by ±1 is 1.
    """
    nums.sort()
    median = nums[len(nums)//2]
    return sum(abs(num - median) for num in nums)
REASON: This step is critical because it alters the cost calculation by adding an unnecessary increment to each operation, leading to an inflated total cost that does not reflect the actual minimum cost required to equalize the array.

TASK: Please solve the programming task below in Python. 

### Question: Find the Index to Maximize Continuous 1s in a Binary Array

#### Objective
You are given a binary array, where each element is either 0 or 1. You need to determine the index of a `0` that, if replaced by `1`, would maximize the length of the longest contiguous subarray of `1`s.

#### Function Specification

```python
def max_ones_index(arr: List[int]) -> int:
    """
    Find the index of 0 to be replaced with 1 to get
    longest continuous sequence of 1s in a binary array.
    If there is no 0 in the array, return -1.
    
    :param arr: List[int] : Input binary array
    :return: int : Index of 0 to be replaced, or -1 if no 0 exists
    """
```

#### Input
- **arr**: A list of integers representing a binary array. (1 ≤ len(arr) ≤ 10^5)

#### Output
- An integer index of the `0` that should be replaced with `1` to maximize the longest contiguous sequence of `1`s.
- If there are multiple possible answers, return the leftmost index.
- If there is no `0` in the array, return `-1`.

#### Constraints
- The array will have at least one element.

#### Example

- Input: `[1, 1, 1, 0, 1, 1, 1, 1, 1, 0, 1, 1, 1]`
- Output: `3`

- Input: `[1, 1, 1, 1, 1]`
- Output: `-1`

- Input: `[0, 1, 0, 1, 1, 1]`
- Output: `2`

#### Notes
- Be sure to handle edge cases such as arrays without any `0`.

Note that the function declaration is def max_ones_index(arr):. 
GT: def max_ones_index(arr):
    """
    Find the index of 0 to be replaced with 1 to get
    longest continuous sequence of 1s in a binary array.

## Task

You are given a matrix `n x m` consisting of numbers that represent the cost of visiting each cell. Your task is to find the minimum cost to reach from the top-left cell to the bottom-right cell of the matrix. You are allowed to move only right or down from a cell.

# Input

- `costMatrix`: A `n` x `m` integer array representing the cost of visiting each cell.
  - `1 <= n, m <= 100`
  - Each element in `costMatrix` is a non-negative integer less than 10^5.

# Output

An integer representing the minimum cost to reach from the top-left cell to the bottom-right cell.

# Example

For 
```
costMatrix =
[
  [1, 3, 1],
  [1, 5, 1],
  [4, 2, 1]
]
```
The output should be `7`.

One of the possible paths to achieve the minimum cost:
```
1 -> 3 -> 1 -> 1 -> 1

Total Cost = 1 + 3 + 1 + 1 + 1 = 7
```

Note that the function declaration is def min_cost_path(costMatrix):.

    """

### **Step 1: Domain Analysis**

Given the constraints:

1. \( x > 0 \)
2. \( 12 - x \geq 0 \implies x \leq 12 \)
3. \( 12x - x^3 \geq 0 \implies x(12 - x^2) \geq 0 \)

Since \( x > 0 \), we need \( 12 - x^2 \geq 0 \implies x^2 \leq 12 \implies x \leq \sqrt{12} = 2\sqrt{3} \approx 3.464 \).

So the domain is:
\[
0 < x \leq 2\sqrt{3}
\]

---

### **Step 2: Main Inequality**

\[

\]

Then,

\]
But also,
\[
y^2 = 12x - x^3
\]
So,
\[
x \sqrt{12 - x} + \sqrt{12x - x^3} \geq 12
\]
